 Review: Amsterdam-based Bob Waardenburg is a self-confessed club-culture vulture who uses this alias to mix up his early influences in punk, psychedelic rock and many forms of electronic sound. Hi latest falls down into a deep, heavy, dubby techno abyss to start with as 'Void' churns with great purpose and tons of reverb. 'Cluster' is automated machines all in sync, whirring, purring, interlocking in their onward journey and 'Wall' brings back those bloody great big kick drums and laces them up with insistent hi-hats and free-roaming synths. The sound designs are high quality and the atmospheres are alluring. 'Filament' closes with waves crashing against Plastikman-like snares that flutter in the wind. … Read more

 Review: Rotterdam's Frenk Dublin has put out a classy stream of dub-rooted sounds in both EP and LP format over the last decade. The title cut will ensure your resting heart rate sinks to below one beat a minute, such is the studiously slow-motion churn of the drums. 'A Distant Echo' and 'The Court of Owls' hardly up the ante; instead the layer in a little more detail - wispy pad here, an infinitely echoing hi-hat there. 'Yesterday' is the brightest thanks to a gossamer-thin synth that lingers above the sedate dub rhythm. … Read more

 Review: Detroit icon and space music obsessive Jeff Mills, under his Millsart alias, continues the Axis Expressionist Series with another chapter drawn from his Every Dog Has Its Day project alongside new unreleased material. This isn't dancefloor techno in the usual sense - it's built for reflection rather than raving and is a meditation this time on hype itself: how it works as a kind of psychological currency, how fleeting attention gets mistaken for lasting substance and how quickly people move from believer to revisionist, chasing that first rush again. True to the series' whole ethos, Mills treats electronic music here as a vehicle for storytelling across dense, thoughtful sounds meant for those with attention spans too many people seem to lack these days.

 X-Break (5:26)
 Review: Reflex Blue enjoyed his first outing on RAND Muzik so much he's back to do it all again. 'Antiacid' opens his account with a bouncy bassline that is given real centre stage. There are some unsettling vocal sounds in the shadows as well as a dusting of percussion and it all has a subtle retro feel. 'Essence Of The Mind' is heads down and locked into the roaming baseline that searches the stereo field with swinging claps and drum fills for company. 'Let It Flow' has the depth and breadth of a dub techno cut but with more elastic rhythm and tough hits. 'X-Break' spins out on old school breakbeats with a speak busting baseline and plenty of sci-fi feels. Great tribute to a bygone sound but done with a contemporary flex. … Read more

 Function Four (5:35)
 Review: The UK's Setaoc Mass has been honing his deep, percussive brand of techno on labels like Soma, Fuse and Fides, so has kept good company in the last couple of years. Now he lands on the Dutch label Vaults and keeps the quality levels high. 'Never Mind' opens with an impish rhythm that isn't showy but has a pent-up mischief and restless to it as sharp hi-hats and detuned synth daubs bring it to life. 'Causeway' has keys that hang unsettlingly above the linear, stripped-back Millsian drums and 'Bumbambow' then has more ruffle and swing to it with a fatalistic lead that descends through the mix. 'Function Four' is a broody, punchy cut with a lead that never rests.

 Fading Coast
 Review: Neapolitan club heritage gets a fresh twist on Global Pulse, where local producer Vincenzo Gargiulo, aka Xqte, taps into the spirit of the early 2000s without getting stuck too much in the past. The 'Orbital South' EP moves between techno, minimal and tech house while tying filtered chords and catchy vocal hooks to funky basslines and crisp, well-judged percussion. There's a warmth that keeps things human, while playful sampling brings a few nice little surprises into the mix. Subtle detail doesn't detract from the direct dancefloor appeal in what is a great window into Naples' enduring scene. … Read more

 The Impatient Heart (3:17)
 Review: Techno stalwart/legend/veteran Marco Bailey offers a first taste of his forthcoming 14-track The Impatient Heart album with this nine-track sampler, and it's a focused dose of tackle. As always with this evergreen producer, his sounds are driven by weighty low end, tightly wound percussion and sequences that build pressure without overcomplicating things. 'Organic Pulse Theory', 'Cellular Rhythm' and 'Fluid Drive' strike a balance between hypnotic repetition, sharp breaks and layers that keep shifting beneath the surface. Elsewhere, the title track rounds out a selection built for different points in the night, from deep, rolling passages to more direct peak-time. Bailey doing what he does best. … Read more

 Station 8
 Review: Jeroen Search doesn't need much introduction. The Dutch producer has been making sharp, machine-funk techno since the mid-90s, and since 2017 has also worked as Echelon. It is a project that behaves like a secret and, according to its own Axis Records notes, officially doesn't exist. After The Five Eyes on Jeff Mills' label, the project lands on Voyager with an eight-tracker of experimental future techno with nothing off limits. It's often sparse and moody, with extraterrestrial signals in place of the usual four-four push, plenty of evocative imagery and a quiet, strange yet deep sense of atmosphere. … Read more

 As I've Always Said (5:26)
 Review: Short Span's eleventh and weightiest release so far, and their first double LP: the debut album from K Wata, a New York producer who's been placing carefully on Anno and City-2 St Giga, and noted for his production contrubtions to Yaeji's With a Hammer and Daytimers collaborative moves. Several of the tracks here were first deployed live before being pulled back and finished in the studio, with drum programming carrying the slow-motion memory of Memphis and Houston trap, but only somewhat tangentially; you wouldn't know it from first listens alone. Vocals from SG on the title track, and 'Go', lend the record an extra layer of filtered heartbreak - "I gotta go" - to the overall sense of shuffly, backroomsy frisson. … Read more

 Coda
 Review: Former punk turned American gay porn maverick J.D. Slater's unreleased Club Inferno scores make for fascinating listening even when removed from their original context. Compiled from three late-90s fisting films, Elbow Deep is the sound Slater embracing loop-based sampling just as the technology was opening up electronic music. 'Grand Fuck' his probably the best tune we've ever heard with Gregorian textures, 'Stallion Funk' has a looser lurch, and 'At Arm's Length' settles into frosty ambience. This is stranger and more atmospheric album than its origins might suggest but one that will keep you coming back for more. … Read more

 Mercy
 Review: American J.D. Slater arrives at this debut with a celebrated but unlikely resume that includes cutting his teeth in the New York punk scene, opening for Talking Heads and Blondie at CBGB with his project Frenzy, before co-founding Raging Stallion Studios and becoming, by reputation, "the Lou Reed of gay porn." Filling Your Silence collects his soundtracks for the label's first two Sexpack films, and reveals there is a real musician underneath that history. 'Seduction' opens on a 90s synthetic pan flute before pulling into wild, trance-like grooves, 'Running with Scissors' tightens the pace with crunchy breakbeats, while 'Mercy' and its second movement lean into a bleepy, early Nightmares on Wax or 808 State vibe. A great artefact from a true polymath. … Read more

 Kangding Ray - "Pacific Coast Highway" (5:54)
 Review: The Non Series label out of Spain has always been a serious player when it comes to mental techno. By mental, we mean heady and cerebral rather than wild, grooves on this label are often long-form, sleek and drawn out for maximum hypnotic effect rather than getting up in your face and demanding physical responses. And that is the case here with a 15th anniversary special that gathers artists from different backgrounds "whose music reflects the spirit and musical identity of the label." Most are debutants and they all impress, from Vladw's slow-burn descent into mind-melting rhythm on 'Ushba' to Kangding Ray's more skittish, IDM-infused 'Pacific Coast Highway'. Pure class. … Read more

 Kasper Bjorke - "Full Circle" (5:30)
 Review: Riotvan began in Leipzig in 2006 as a small party to give Peter Invasion's band a home, became a label in 2012, then grew with Panthera Krause's arrival in 2017. Twenty years on, 240 months of friends and family are on one record across ten cool tracks. Riotvan's own '240 Months of Riotvan,' with Baby Mango, opens on a whacked-out art-tech vibe and is followed by more strobe-lit synth jams by Man Power, Shubostar's dreamy and psychedelic chugger 'Down in Mexico' and Jennifer Touch's darker, more left-of-centre 'Bones (Retouch)'. It's a colourful, wide-ranging fusion of techno, disco and electronic pop that does its own thing. … Read more

 Stanislav Tolkachev - "Eyes Don't Close" (5:22)
 Review: Techno has always been the sound of the future and this new compilation from the young Biometic Audio label leans into that with six cuts from six on-form artists. Prisoner takes none with the blisteringly deep, driving attack of his 'Sending' then Xavo gets for brushed metal textures on his hypnotic, levitating gem. Basic/Fael/Yuki bring some broken beats and gurgling machine sounds to 'She Spoke In A Detached Way' and Giri ramps up the dystopian tension wit more wayward electronics on 'Some Animals Are More Equal'. DJ Shufflemaster's 'Deadly Drive' is clanking industrial loops interspersed with alien sounds and Stanislav Tolkachev gets more wonky, unsigned and neanderthal on 'Eyes Don't Close.' … Read more
